–

Construct a
consistency graph

G_{i}
over
\mathcal{P}
, where the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
is included in
G_{i}
, if
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
is received from the broadcast of
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively, either through the regular-mode or fall-back mode.

\bullet

Phase IV — Checking for an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
:
\mathsf{D}
does the following in its consistency graph

G_{\mathsf{D}}
at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.

–

Remove edges incident with
P_{i}
, if
\texttt{NOK}(i,j,q_{ij})
is received from the broadcast of
P_{i}
through regular-mode
and
q_{ij}\neq Q(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
.

–

Set
\mathcal{W}=\{P_{i}:\mathsf{deg}(P_{i})\geq n-t_{s}\}
, where
\mathsf{deg}(P_{i})
denotes the degree of
P_{i}
in
G_{\mathsf{D}}
.

–

Remove
P_{i}
from
\mathcal{W}
, if
P_{i}
is
not
incident with at least
n-t_{s}
parties in
\mathcal{W}
. Repeat this step till no more parties can be
removed from
\mathcal{W}
.

–

Run algorithm
\mathsf{AlgStar}
on
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
, where
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
denotes the subgraph of
G_{\mathsf{D}}
induced by the vertices in
\mathcal{W}
.
If an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
, say
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, is obtained, then broadcast
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.

\bullet

Local Computation — Verifying and Accepting
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
:
Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
does the following at time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.

–

If a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is received from
\mathsf{D}
’s broadcast through regular-mode, then
accept
it if following
were true
at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
:

–

There exist no
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
, such that

\texttt{NOK}(j,k,q_{jk})
and
\texttt{NOK}(k,j,q_{kj})
messages
were
received from the broadcast of
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively through regular-mode,
where
q_{jk}\neq q_{kj}
.

–

In the consistency graph
G_{i}
,

\mathsf{deg}(P_{j})\geq n-t_{s}
for all
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
.

–

In the consistency graph
G_{i}
, every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
has
edges with at least
n-t_{s}
parties from
\mathcal{W}
.

–

(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})

was
an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
in the induced graph
G_{i}[\mathcal{W}]
.

–

For every
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
where the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
is present in
G_{i}
, the
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
messages
were
received from the
broadcast of
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively, through regular-mode.

\bullet

Phase V — Deciding Whether to Go for an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
:
At time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
participates in an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
with input

b_{i}=0
if a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
was accepted, else with input
b_{i}=1
, and
waits for time
T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

\bullet

Local Computation — Computing WPS-share Through
\mathcal{W}
:
If the output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is 0, then each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
computes its
WPS-Share

s_{i}
(initially set to
\bot
) as follows.

–

If a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is not yet received then wait till a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is received from
\mathsf{D}
’s broadcast through fall-back mode.

–

If
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
, then output
s_{i}=q_{i}(0)
.

–

Else, initialise a support set
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
to
\emptyset
.
If
q_{ji}
is received from
P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}
, include
q_{ji}
to
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
. Keep executing
\mathsf{OEC}(t_{s},t_{s},\mathcal{SS}_{i})
,
till a
t_{s}
-degree polynomial, say
q_{i}(\cdot)
, is obtained. Then, output
s_{i}=q_{i}(0)
.

\bullet

Phase VI — Broadcasting an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
: If the output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is 1, then
\mathsf{D}
does the following.

–

After every update in the consistency graph
G_{\mathsf{D}}
, run
\mathsf{AlgStar}
on
G_{\mathsf{D}}
. If an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
, say

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
, is obtained, then broadcast
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
.

\bullet

Local Computation — Computing WPS-share Through
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
: If the output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is 1, then each
P_{i}
does the following to compute
its
WPS-Share
.

–

Waits till an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
is obtained from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
, either through regular or fall-back mode. Upon receiving, wait
till
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
becomes an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in
G_{i}
.

–

If
P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, then output
s_{i}=q_{i}(0)
.

–

Else, initialise a support set
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
to
\emptyset
.
If
q_{ji}
is received from
P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, include
q_{ji}
to
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
. Keep executing
\mathsf{OEC}(t_{s},t_{s},\mathcal{SS}_{i})
,
till a
t_{s}
-degree polynomial, say
q_{i}(\cdot)
, is obtained. Then, output
s_{i}=q_{i}(0)
.

Figure 3
:
The best-of-both-worlds weak polynomial-sharing protocol for a single polynomial.

We next proceed to prove the properties of the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
. We begin with showing that if
\mathsf{D}
is
honest
, then the adversary does not learn
anything additional about
q(\cdot)
, irrespective of the network type.

Lemma 4.1

(
t_{s}
-Privacy)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is honest, then irrespective of the network type, the view of the adversary remains independent of
q(\cdot)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
honest
. We consider the worst case scenario, when the adversary controls up to
t_{s}
parties.
We claim that throughout the protocol, the adversary learns at most
t_{s}
univariate polynomials lying on
Q(x,y)
.
Since
Q(x,y)
is a random

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree- symmetric-bivariate polynomial, it then follows from Lemma
2.2
that the view of the adversary will be independent of

q(\cdot)
.
We next proceed to prove the claim.

Corresponding to every
corrupt

P_{i}
, the adversary learns
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
. Corresponding to every
honest

P_{i}
, the adversary learns
t_{s}
distinct points on

P_{i}
’s univariate polynomial
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
, through the pair-wise consistency checks. However, these points were already included in the view of the adversary
(through the univariate polynomials under adversary’s control).
Hence no additional information about the polynomials of the honest parties is revealed during the pair-wise consistency checks.
Furthermore, no
honest

P_{i}
ever broadcasts
\texttt{NOK}(i,j,q_{i}(\alpha_{j}))
, corresponding to
any
honest

P_{j}
. This is because the pair-wise consistency check will always pass for every pair of
honest
parties.
∎

We next prove the correctness property in a
synchronous
network.

Lemma 4.2

(
t_{s}
-Correctness)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is honest and the network is synchronous, then each honest
P_{i}
outputs

q(\alpha_{i})
at time
T_{\mathsf{WPS}}=2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
honest
and the network be
synchronous
with up to
t_{s}
corruptions.
During phase I, every
honest
party
P_{j}
receives
q_{j}(x)=Q(x,\alpha_{j})
from
\mathsf{D}
within time
\Delta
.
Hence during phase II,
every
honest

P_{j}
sends
q_{jk}
to every
P_{k}
, which takes at most
\Delta
time to be delivered.
Hence, by time
2\Delta
, every
honest

P_{j}

receives
q_{kj}
from every
honest

P_{k}
, such that
q_{kj}=q_{j}(\alpha_{k})
holds.
Consequently, during phase III, every
honest

P_{j}
broadcasts
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
corresponding to every
honest

P_{k}
, and vice versa. From the
t_{s}
-validity

property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network,
it follows that every honest
P_{i}
receives
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
from the broadcast of every
honest

P_{j}
and every
honest

P_{k}
respectively,
through regular-mode,
at
time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Hence, the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
will be added to the consistency
graph
G_{i}
, corresponding to every honest
P_{j},P_{k}
.
Furthermore, from the
t_{s}
-consistency
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
, the graph
G_{i}
will be the same for every honest party
P_{i}
(including
\mathsf{D}
)
at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Moreover,
if
\mathsf{D}
receives an
incorrect

\texttt{NOK}(i,j,q_{ij})
message from the broadcast of any
corrupt

P_{i}
through regular-mode at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, where
q_{ij}\neq Q(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
, then
\mathsf{D}
removes all the edges incident with

P_{i}
in
\mathsf{D}
’s consistency graph
G_{\mathsf{D}}
. Dealer

\mathsf{D}
then computes the set
\mathcal{W}
, and all
honest
parties will be present in
\mathcal{W}
. Moreover, the honest parties will
form a clique of size at least
n-t_{s}
in the induced subgraph
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}

and
\mathsf{D}
will find an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
, say
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, in
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
and broadcast
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
during phase IV.
By the
t_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network,
all honest parties will receive
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
through regular-mode at time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Moreover, all honest parties will
accept

(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
and participate with input
0
in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Hence, by the
t_{s}
-validity
and
t_{s}
-guaranteed liveness
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network,

every
honest party obtains the output
0
in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
, by time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Now, consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. Since
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
, party
P_{i}
outputs
s_{i}=q_{i}(0)=Q(0,\alpha_{i})=q(\alpha_{i})
.
∎

We next prove the correctness property in an
asynchronous
network.

Lemma 4.3

(
t_{a}
-Correctness)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is honest and network is asynchronous, then almost-surely,
each honest
P_{i}
eventually outputs
q(\alpha_{i})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
honest
and network be
asynchronous
with up to
t_{a}
corruptions. We first note that every honest party participates with some input in the instance
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
at local time

2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Hence
from the
t_{a}
-almost-surely liveness
and
t_{a}
-consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in an
asynchronous
network,
it follows that almost-surely, the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
eventually generates
some common output, for all honest parties. Now there are two possible cases:

–

The output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is 0
: From the
t_{a}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network,
it follows that at least one
honest
party, say
P_{h}
, participated with input
0
during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
This implies that
P_{h}

has accepted a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
at local
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, which is
received from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
, through
regular-mode
.
Hence, by the
t_{a}
-weak validity
and
t_{a}
-fallback validity
properties
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network,
all honest parties will eventually receive
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
and
accept
the triplet.
This is because
the consistency graphs of all honest parties will eventually have all the edges which were present in the consistency graph

G_{h}
of
P_{h}
, at time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
We claim that
every honest

P_{i}
will eventually get
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
.
This will imply that eventually, every
honest

P_{i}
outputs
s_{i}=Q(0,\alpha_{i})=q(\alpha_{i})
.
To prove the claim, consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. There are two possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
: In this case,
P_{i}
already has received
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
from
\mathsf{D}
.

–

P_{i}\not\in\mathcal{W}
:
In this case, there will be at least
n-t_{s}>2t_{s}+t_{a}
parties in
\mathcal{F}
, of which at most
t_{a}
could be
corrupt
.
Since
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
is a
t_{s}
-degree polynomial and
t_{s}<|\mathcal{F}|-2t_{a}
,
from Lemma
A.1
, it follows that by applying the OEC procedure on the common points on the
polynomials
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
, received from the parties in
\mathcal{F}
, party
P_{i}
will eventually obtain

Q(x,\alpha_{i})
.

–

The output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is 1
: Since
\mathsf{D}
is
honest
, every pair of honest parties
P_{j},P_{k}
eventually
broadcast
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
messages respectively, as the pair-wise consistency check between them will eventually be successful.
From the
t_{a}
-weak validity
and
t_{a}
-fallback validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
, these messages are eventually delivered to every honest party.
Also from the
t_{a}
-weak consistency
and
t_{a}
-fallback consistency

of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network, any
OK
message which is received by
\mathsf{D}
from the broadcast of any
corrupt
party,
will be eventually received by every other honest party as well.
As there will be at least
n-t_{a}
honest parties, a clique of size at least
n-t_{a}
will eventually form in the consistency graph of every honest party.
Hence
\mathsf{D}
will eventually find an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
, say
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
, in its consistency graph and broadcast it. From
the
t_{a}
-weak validity
and
t_{a}
-fallback validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
, this star will be eventually delivered to every honest party.
Moreover,
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
will be eventually an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in every honest party’s consistency graph.
We claim that
every honest

P_{i}
will eventually get
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
.
This will imply that eventually, every
honest

P_{i}
outputs
s_{i}=Q(0,\alpha_{i})=q(\alpha_{i})
.
To prove the claim, consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. There are two possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case,
P_{i}
already has
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
, received from
\mathsf{D}
.

–

P_{i}\notin\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case, there will be at least
n-t_{a}>3t_{s}
parties in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, of which at most
t_{a}
could be
corrupt
, where
t_{a}<t_{s}
.
Since
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
is a
t_{s}
-degree polynomial and
t_{s}<|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|-2t_{a}
, from Lemma
A.1

it follows that by applying the OEC procedure on the common points on the
polynomial
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
, received from the parties in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, party
P_{i}
will eventually obtain

Q(x,\alpha_{i})
.

∎

We next proceed to prove the weak commitment properties for a
corrupt

\mathsf{D}
. However, before that we prove a helping lemma.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let the network be synchronous and let
\mathsf{D}
be corrupt in the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
.
If any one honest party receives a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode and accepts it at time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
,
then all the following hold.

–

All honest parties in
\mathcal{W}
receive their respective
t_{s}
-degree univariate polynomials from
\mathsf{D}
, within time
\Delta
.

–

The univariate polynomials
q_{i}(x)
of all honest parties
P_{i}
in the set
\mathcal{W}
lie on a unique

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.

–

Within time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, every honest party accepts
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
corrupt
and network be
synchronous
with up to
t_{s}
corruptions. As per the lemma condition, let
P_{h}
be an
honest
party, who
receives a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode and accepts
it at time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Then from the protocol steps, the following must be
true for
P_{h}
at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
:

–

There does not exist any

P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
,
such that
\texttt{NOK}(j,k,q_{jk})
and
\texttt{NOK}(k,j,q_{kj})
messages are received by
P_{h}
, from the broadcast of

P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively through regular-mode, where
q_{jk}\neq q_{kj}
.

–

In
P_{h}
’s consistency graph
G_{h}
,
\mathsf{deg}(P_{j})\geq n-t_{s}
for all
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
and
P_{j}
has edges with at least

n-t_{s}
parties from
\mathcal{W}
.

–

(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
constitutes an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
in the induced subgraph
G_{h}[\mathcal{W}]
, such that for every

P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
where the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
is present in
G_{h}
, the messages
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
are
received by
P_{h}
, from the broadcast of

P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively, through regular-mode.

We prove the first part of the lemma through a contradiction. So let
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
be an
honest
party, who receives its

t_{s}
-degree univariate polynomial, say
q_{j}(x)
, from
\mathsf{D}
at time
\Delta+\delta
, where

\delta>0
. Moreover, let
P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
be an
honest
party, different from
P_{j}
(note that there are at least
n-2t_{s}

honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
).
As stated above, at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, party
P_{h}
receives the message
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
from the broadcast of
P_{k}
through regular-mode.
From the protocol steps,
P_{j}
waits till its local time becomes a multiple of
\Delta
, before it sends the points on its polynomial to other parties for pair-wise consistency tests.
Hence,
P_{j}
must have started sending the points after time
c\cdot\Delta
, where
c\geq 2
.
Since the network is
synchronous
, the point
q_{jk}=q_{j}(\alpha_{k})

must have been received by
P_{k}
by time
(c+1)\cdot\Delta
.
Moreover, from the protocol steps, even if
P_{k}
receives these points at time
T
, where
c\cdot\Delta<T<(c+1)\cdot\Delta
,
it waits till time
(c+1)\cdot\Delta
, before broadcasting the
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
message.
Since
P_{k}
is
honest
, from the
t_{s}
-validity
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network,
it will take
exactly

T_{\mathsf{BC}}
time for the message
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
to be received through regular-mode, once it is broadcast.
This implies that
P_{h}
will receive the message
\texttt{OK}(k,j)

at time
(c+1)\cdot\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, where
(c+1)>2
. However, this is a contradiction,
since the
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
message has been received by
P_{h}
at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.

To prove the second part of the lemma, we will show that
the univariate polynomials
q_{j}(x)
of all the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
are pair-wise consistent.
Since there are at least
n-2t_{s}>t_{s}

honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
,
from Lemma
2.1
, it follows
that the univariate polynomials
q_{j}(x)

of all the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
lie on a unique
(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
So consider an arbitrary pair of
honest
parties
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
. From the first part of the lemma, both
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
must have received their
respective univariate polynomials

q_{j}(x)

and
q_{k}(x)
by time
\Delta
. This further implies that
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
must have received the points
q_{kj}=q_{k}(\alpha_{j})
and

q_{jk}=q_{j}(\alpha_{k})
respectively by time

2\Delta
. If
q_{kj}\neq q_{jk}
, then
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
would broadcast
\texttt{NOK}(j,k,q_{jk})
and
\texttt{NOK}(k,j,q_{kj})

messages respectively, at time
2\Delta
.
Consequently, from the
t_{s}
-validity
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network,

P_{h}
will receive these messages through regular-mode at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Consequently,
P_{h}

will not
accept
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, which is a contradiction.

To prove the third part of the lemma, we note that since
P_{h}
has received

(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode at time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, it implies
that
\mathsf{D}
must have started broadcasting
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
latest at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
This is because it takes
T_{\mathsf{BC}}
time for the regular-mode of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
to produce an
output. From the

t_{s}
-consistency
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network, it follows that every honest party will also receive
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})

from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode,
at time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Since at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, party
P_{h}
has received the
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
messages
through regular-mode
from the broadcast of
every
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
where
(P_{j},P_{k})
is an edge in
P_{h}
’s consistency graph,
it follows that these messages started getting broadcast, latest at time
2\Delta
.
From the
t_{s}
-validity

and
t_{s}
-consistency
properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network,
it follows that every honest party receives these broadcast messages through regular-mode at time

2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Hence
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
will constitute an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
in the induced subgraph
G_{i}[\mathcal{W}]
of every honest party
P_{i}
’s
consistency-graph at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}

and consequently, every honest party accepts
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.
∎

Now based on the above helping lemma, we proceed to prove the weak commitment properties of the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
.

Lemma 4.5

(
t_{s}
-Weak Commitment)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is corrupt and network is synchronous, then either no honest party computes any output or there exists some

t_{s}
-degree polynomial, say
q^{\star}(\cdot)
, such that all the following hold.

–

There are at least
t_{s}+1
honest parties
P_{i}
who
output the WPS-shares
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.

–

If any honest
P_{j}
outputs a WPS-share
s_{j}\in\mathbb{F}
, then

s_{j}=q^{\star}(\alpha_{j})
holds.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
corrupt
and network be
synchronous
with up to
t_{s}
corruptions.
If no honest party outputs any wps-share, then the lemma holds trivially. So consider the case when some honest party
outputs a wps-share, which is an element of
\mathbb{F}
.
Now, there are two possible cases.

–

At time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, at least one honest party, say
P_{h}
, accepts a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, received
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode
:
In this case, from Lemma
4.4
, at time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, every honest party will accept
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.
Hence every honest party participates in the instance of

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
with input
0
. From the
t_{s}
-validity
and
t_{s}
-guaranteed liveness
properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network,
all honest parties will get the output
0
during the instance of

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
by time
T_{\mathsf{WPS}}=2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
. From Lemma
4.4
,
the univariate polynomials of all the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
will lie on some

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
Let
q^{\star}(\cdot)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}Q^{\star}(0,y)
.
Now consider an arbitrary
honest

party
P_{i}
, who outputs a wps-share
s_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
.
We want to show that the
condition
s_{i}=q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
holds.
And there are at least
t_{s}+1
such
honest
parties
P_{i}
who output their wps-share.
There are two possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
: From the protocol steps,
P_{i}
sets
s_{i}=Q^{\star}(0,\alpha_{i})
,
which is the same as
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.
Since
\mathcal{W}
contains at least
t_{s}+t_{a}+1
honest parties, this also shows that at least
t_{s}+1
honest parties
P_{i}
output
their respective wps-share
s_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
, which is the same as
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.

–

P_{i}\not\in\mathcal{W}
:
In this case,
P_{i}
sets
s_{i}=q_{i}(0)
, where
q_{i}(\cdot)
is a
t_{s}
-degree univariate polynomial,
obtained by applying the OEC procedure with
d=t=t_{s}
,
on the values
q_{ji}
, received from the parties
P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}
, during the pair-wise consistency checks.
Note that as part of OEC (see the proof of Lemma
A.1
),
party
P_{i}
verifies that at least
2t_{s}+1

q_{ji}
values from the parties in
\mathcal{F}
lie on

q_{i}(\cdot)
. Now out of these
2t_{s}+1

q_{ji}
values, at least
t_{s}+1
values are from the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{F}
. Furthermore, these

q_{ji}
values from the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{F}
are the same as
Q^{\star}(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j})
, which is equal to

Q^{\star}(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
and uniquely determine

Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
; the last property holds since
Q^{\star}(x,y)
is a symmetric bivariate polynomial.
This automatically implies that
q_{i}(x)
is the
same
as
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
and hence

s_{i}=q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
, since two different
t_{s}
-degree polynomials can have at most
t_{s}
common values.

–

At time
2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+2\Delta
, no honest party has accepted any
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
:
This implies that all honest parties participate in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
with input
1
. So by the
t_{s}
-validity
and
t_{s}
-guaranteed liveness
of

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network,
all honest parties obtain the output
1
in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
. Let
P_{h}
be the
first honest
party who outputs a wps-share, consisting of an element
from
\mathbb{F}
.
This means that
P_{h}
has received a pair
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
,
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
, such that
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
constitutes an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}

in
P_{h}
’s consistency graph. By the
t_{s}
-consistency
and
t_{s}
-fallback consistency
properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous network,

all
honest parties receive
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})

from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
. Moreover, since
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
constitutes an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in
P_{h}
’s consistency graph, it will also constitute an

(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in every other honest party’s consistency graph as well. This is because the
\texttt{OK}(\star,\star)
messages which are received by

P_{h}
from the broadcast of the various parties in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, are also received by every other honest party, either through regular-mode
or fallback-mode.
The last property follows from the
t_{s}
-validity,
t_{s}
-consistency
and
t_{s}
-fallback consistency
properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous network.
Since
|\mathcal{E}^{\prime}|\geq n-2t_{a}>2t_{s}+(t_{s}-t_{a})>2t_{s}
, it follows that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
has at least
t_{s}+1

honest
parties
P_{i}
, whose
univariate polynomials
q_{i}(x)
are pair-wise consistent. Hence, from Lemma
2.1
, these univariate polynomials
lie on a unique

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
Similarly, since the univariate polynomial
q_{i}(x)
of every
honest
party in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is pair-wise consistent with the univariate polynomials
q_{j}(x)

of the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
,
it implies that the univariate polynomials
q_{i}(x)

of all the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
also lie on
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
Let
q^{\star}(\cdot)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}Q^{\star}(0,y)
. We show that
every honest

P_{i}

outputs a wps-share, which is the same as

q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
. For this it is enough to show that each honest
P_{i}
gets
q_{i}(x)=Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
,
as
P_{i}
outputs
q_{i}(0)
as its wps-share, which will be then same as
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.
Consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. There are two possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case,
P_{i}
already has
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
, received from
\mathsf{D}
.

–

P_{i}\notin\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case, there will be
n-t_{a}>3t_{s}
parties in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, of which at most
t_{s}
could be corrupt.
Moreover,
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
is a
t_{s}
-degree polynomial and
t_{s}<|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|-2t_{s}
holds.
Hence from the properties of OEC (Lemma
A.1
), by applying the OEC procedure on the common points on the
polynomial
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
received from the parties in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, party
P_{i}
will compute

Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
.

∎

We finally prove the commitment property in an
asynchronous
network.

Lemma 4.6

(
t_{a}
-Strong Commitment)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is corrupt and network is asynchronous, then either no honest party computes any output or there exist some

t_{s}
-degree polynomial, say
q^{\star}(\cdot)
, such that almost-surely, every honest
P_{i}
eventually outputs a wps-share

q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.
11
11

11

Note that
unlike
the synchronous network,
the commitment property in the
asynchronous
network is
strong
. That is, if at all any honest party outputs a wps-share, then
all
the honest
parties are guaranteed to eventually output their wps-shares.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
corrupt
and network be
asynchronous
with up to
t_{a}
corruptions. If no honest party computes
any output, then the lemma holds trivially. So consider the case when some honest party
outputs a wps-share, consisting of an element of
\mathbb{F}
. We note that every honest party participates with some input in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}

at local time

2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Hence, from the
t_{a}
-almost-surely liveness
and
t_{a}
-consistency
properties
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network,
almost-surely, all honest parties eventually compute a common output during
the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
. Now there are two possible cases:

–

The output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is 0
:
From the
t_{a}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network, it implies that
at least one honest party, say
P_{h}
, participated with input
0
during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
This further implies that at local time
2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, party
P_{h}

has accepted a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, which has been received by
P_{h}

from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
, through regular-mode.
Hence, by the
t_{a}
-weak consistency
and
t_{a}
-fallback consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in
the
asynchronous
network,
all honest parties will eventually receive
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
.
There will be at least
n-2t_{s}-t_{a}>t_{s}

honest
parties in
\mathcal{E}
, whose univariate polynomials
q_{i}(x)
are pair-wise consistent and hence
from Lemma
2.1
lie on a unique

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
. Similarly, the univariate polynomial
q_{j}(x)
of every
honest

P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}
will be pair-wise consistent with the univariate polynomials
q_{i}(x)
of all the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{E}
and hence lie on

Q^{\star}(x,y)
as well. Let
q^{\star}(\cdot)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}Q^{\star}(0,y)
.
We claim that
every honest

P_{i}
will eventually get
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
.
This will imply that eventually every
honest

P_{i}
outputs the wps-share
s_{i}=Q^{\star}(0,\alpha_{i})=q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.
To prove the claim, consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. There are three possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
and
P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
: In this case,
P_{i}
already has
q_{i}(x)
, received from
\mathsf{D}
. And since
P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
, the condition

q_{i}(x)=Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
holds.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
and
P_{i}\not\in\mathcal{F}
: In this case,
P_{i}
already has
q_{i}(x)
, received from
\mathsf{D}
.
Since
|\mathcal{W}|\geq n-t_{s}
and
|\mathcal{F}|\geq n-t_{s}
,
|\mathcal{W}\cap\mathcal{F}|\geq n-2t_{s}>t_{s}+t_{a}
.
From the protocol steps, the polynomial
q_{i}(x)
is pair-wise consistent with the polynomial
q_{j}(x)
of at least
n-t_{s}
parties
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}

(since
P_{i}
has edges with at least
n-t_{s}
parties
P_{j}
within
\mathcal{W}
). Now among these
n-t_{s}
parties, at least
n-2t_{s}
parties will be from
\mathcal{F}
, of which
at least
n-2t_{s}-t_{a}>t_{s}
parties will be
honest
. Hence,
q_{i}(x)
is pair-wise consistent with the
q_{j}(x)
polynomials of at least
t_{s}+1

honest
parties
P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}
. Now since the
q_{j}(x)
polynomial of all the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{F}
lie on
Q^{\star}(x,y)
, it implies that

q_{i}(x)=Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
holds.

–

P_{i}\not\in\mathcal{W}
:
In this case, there will be
n-t_{s}
parties in
\mathcal{F}
, of which at most
t_{a}
could be
corrupt
.
Since
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
is a
t_{s}
-degree polynomial, and
t_{s}<|\mathcal{F}|-2t_{a}
, from Lemma
A.1

it follows that by applying the OEC procedure on the common points on the

Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
polynomial received from the parties in
\mathcal{F}
, party
P_{i}
will eventually obtain

Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
.

–

The output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is 1
: Let
P_{h}
be the
first honest
party, who outputs a wps-share.
This means that
P_{h}
has received a pair, say
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
,
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
, such that
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
constitutes an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}

in
P_{h}
’s consistency graph. By the
t_{a}
-weak consistency
and
t_{a}
-fallback consistency
properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network,

all
honest parties eventually receive
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})

from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
. Moreover, since the consistency graphs are constructed based on the
broadcast
OK
messages and since

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
constitutes an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in
P_{h}
’s consistency graph, from the
t_{a}
-weak validity,
t_{a}
-fallback validity,
t_{a}
-weak consistency and

t_{a}
-fallback consistency

properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network, the pair

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
will eventually constitute an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in every honest party’s consistency graph.
Since
|\mathcal{E}^{\prime}|\geq n-2t_{a}>2t_{s}+(t_{s}-t_{a})>2t_{s}
, it follows that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
has at least
t_{s}+1

honest
parties, whose
univariate polynomials
q_{i}(x)
are pair-wise consistent and hence from Lemma
2.1
, lie on a unique
degree-
(t_{s},t_{s})
symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
Similarly, since the univariate polynomial
q_{j}(x)
of every
honest
party
P_{j}
in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is pair-wise consistent with the univariate polynomials

q_{i}(x)
of the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
,
it implies that the univariate polynomial
q_{j}(x)
of all the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
also lie on
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
Let
q^{\star}(\cdot)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}Q^{\star}(0,y)
. We show that
every honest

P_{i}
eventually
outputs
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
as its wps-share. For this it is enough to show that each honest
P_{i}
eventually gets

q_{i}(x)=Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
,
as
P_{i}
outputs
q_{i}(0)
as its wps-share, which will be the same as
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.
Consider an arbitrary
honest

P_{i}
. There are two possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case,
P_{i}
already has
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
, received from
\mathsf{D}
.

–

P_{i}\notin\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case,
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
has at least
n-t_{a}>3t_{s}
parties, of which at most
t_{a}
could be corrupt.
Since
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
is a
t_{s}
-degree polynomial and
t_{s}<|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|-2t_{a}
, from Lemma
A.1
,
it follows that by applying the OEC procedure on the common points on the
polynomial
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
received from the parties in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, party
P_{i}
will eventually obtain

Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
.

∎

Lemma 4.7
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
incurs a communication of
\mathcal{O}(n^{4}\log{|\mathbb{F}|})
bits from the honest parties
and invokes
1
instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

Proof.

In the protocol,
\mathsf{D}
sends a
t_{s}
-degree univariate polynomial to every party. As part of the pair-wise consistency checks, each pair of parties exchange
2

field elements. In addition, an
honest
party may broadcast an

NOK
message, corresponding to every other party. As part of the
NOK
message,
the honest party also broadcasts the corresponding common point on its univariate polynomial. Each such common point can be represented by
\log{|\mathbb{F}|}
bits.
The communication complexity now follows from the communication complexity of the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
(see Theorem
3.5
).
∎

We next discuss the modifications needed in the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
, if the input for
\mathsf{D}
consists of
L
number of

t_{s}
-degree polynomials.

\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
for
L
Polynomials:

If
\mathsf{D}
has
L
polynomials as input in protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
,
then it embeds them
into
L
random

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomials and distributes the univariate polynomials lying on these bivariate polynomials,
to the respective parties. The parties then perform the
pair-wise consistency tests, by exchanging their supposedly common points on the bivariate polynomials.
However,
P_{i}
broadcasts a
single

\texttt{OK}(i,j)
message for
P_{j}
, if the pair-wise consistency test is positive for
all
the

L
supposedly common values between
P_{i}
and
P_{j}
.
On the other hand, if the test fails for
any
of the
L
supposedly common values, then

P_{i}
broadcasts a
single

NOK
message, corresponding to the
least indexed
common value for which the test fails.
Hence, instead of constructing
L
consistency graphs,
a
single
consistency graph is
constructed by each party. As a result,
\mathsf{D}
finds a
single

(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
triplet and broadcast it.
Similarly, a
single
instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is used to decide whether any
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is accepted.
Finally, if no
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
triplet is found and broadcast, then
\mathsf{D}
looks for a
single

(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
and broadcasts it.

To void repetition,
we skip the formal details of the modified protocol and the proof of its properties,
as stated in Theorem
4.8
.

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
n>3t_{s}+t_{a}
and let
\mathsf{D}
has

L
number of
t_{s}
-degree polynomials
q^{(1)}(\cdot),\ldots,q^{(L)}(\cdot)
as input for
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
, where
L\geq 1
. Moreover, let

T_{\mathsf{WPS}}=2\Delta+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Then protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
achieves the following properties.

–

If
\mathsf{D}
is honest then the following hold.

–

t_{s}
-correctness: In a synchronous network, each (honest)
P_{i}
outputs

\{q(\alpha_{i})\}_{\ell=1,\ldots,L}
at time
T_{\mathsf{WPS}}
.

–

t_{a}
-correctness: In an asynchronous network, almost-surely, each (honest)
P_{i}
eventually outputs

\{q(\alpha_{i})\}_{\ell=1,\ldots,L}
.

–

t_{s}
-privacy: Irrespective of the network type, the view of the adversary remains independent of
the polynomials
q^{(1)}(\cdot),\ldots,q^{(L)}(\cdot)
.

–

If
\mathsf{D}
is corrupt, then either no
honest party computes any output or there exist
L
number
of
t_{s}
-degree polynomials, say
\{{q^{\star}}(\cdot)\}_{\ell=1,\ldots,L}
, such that the following hold.

–

t_{s}
-Weak Commitment: In a synchronous network, at least
t_{s}+1
honest parties
P_{i}

output wps-shares
\{{q^{\star}}(\alpha_{i})\}_{\ell=1,\ldots,L}
. Moreover, if any honest
P_{j}
outputs wps-shares

s^{(1)}_{j},\ldots,\allowbreak s^{(L)}_{j}\in\mathbb{F}
, then

s_{j}={q^{\star}}(\alpha_{j})
holds for
\ell=1,\ldots,L
.

–

t_{a}
-Strong Commitment: In an asynchronous network, almost-surely, each (honest)
P_{i}

eventually outputs
\{{q^{\star}}(\alpha_{i})\}_{\ell=1,\ldots,L}
as wps-shares.

–

Irrespective of the network type, the protocol incurs a communication of
\mathcal{O}(n^{2}L\log{|\mathbb{F}|}+n^{4}\log{|\mathbb{F}|})

bits from the honest parties and invokes
1
instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

4.2
The VSS Protocol

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
fails to serve as a VSS because if
\mathsf{D}
is
corrupt
and the network is
synchronous
,
then the (honest) parties
outside

\mathcal{W}
may not obtain their required shares, lying on
\mathsf{D}
’s committed polynomials.
Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
(see Fig
4
) fixes this shortcoming. For ease of understanding, we present the protocol assuming

\mathsf{D}
has a single
t_{s}
-degree polynomial as input and later discuss the modifications needed when
\mathsf{D}
has
L
such polynomials.
The protocol has two “layers” of communication involved.
The first layer is similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
and identifies whether
the parties accepted some
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
within a specified time-out, such that the polynomials of all
honest parties in
\mathcal{W}
lie on a single
(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
If some
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is accepted, then the second layer of communication (which is coupled with the first layer) enables
even the (honest) parties outside
\mathcal{W}
to get their corresponding polynomials lying on
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.

In more detail, to perform the pair-wise
consistency check of the polynomials received from
\mathsf{D}
, each
P_{j}
upon receiving
q_{j}(x)
from
\mathsf{D}
,
shares the polynomial
q_{j}(x)
by invoking an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
as a dealer. Any party
P_{i}
who computes a WPS-Share in this instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
either broadcasts
an
OK
or
NOK
message for
P_{j}
, depending on whether the WPS-share lies on the polynomial which
P_{i}
has received from
\mathsf{D}
.
The rest of the steps for computing
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
and accepting it remains the same.
If some
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is accepted,
then any
P_{i}

outside

\mathcal{W}
computes its polynomial lying on
Q^{\star}(x,y)
as follows:
P_{i}
checks for a subset
\mathcal{SS}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{F}

of
t_{s}+1
parties

P_{j}
, such that
P_{i}
is able to compute its WPS-share in the
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
instance invoked by
P_{j}
as a dealer.
Such an
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
is bound to exist as there are at least
t_{s}+1

honest
parties in
\mathcal{F}
who are always included in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
.
While the WPS-shares corresponding to the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
will be the common points on
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
, the same holds
even for
corrupt
parties in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
. This is because in order to be included in
\mathcal{F}
,
such parties are “forced” to share
polynomials lying on
Q^{\star}(x,y)
, in their respective instances
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
. Now using the WPS-shares corresponding to the parties in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, party
P_{i}
will be able to compute

Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
and hence, its share.

\justify

\bullet

Phase I — Sending Polynomials
:
\mathsf{D}
on having the input
q(\cdot)
, chooses a random
(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial

Q(x,y)
such that
Q(0,y)=q(\cdot)
and sends
q_{i}(x)=Q(x,\alpha_{i})
to each party
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
.

\bullet

Phase II — Exchanging Common Values
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
, upon receiving a

t_{s}
-degree polynomial
q_{i}(x)
from
\mathsf{D}
,
waits till the current local time becomes a multiple of
\Delta
and then does the following.

–

Act as a dealer and invoke an instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(i)}
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
with input
q_{i}(x)
.

–

For
j=1,\ldots,n
, participate in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
, if invoked by
P_{j}
as a dealer,
and wait for time
T_{\mathsf{WPS}}
.

\bullet

Phase III — Publicly Declaring the Results of Pair-Wise Consistency Test
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}

waits till the local time becomes a multiple of
\Delta

and then does the following.

–

If a WPS-share
q_{ji}
is computed during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
and
q_{i}(x)
has been received from
\mathsf{D}
, then:

–

Broadcast
\texttt{OK}(i,j)
, if
q_{ji}=q_{i}(\alpha_{j})
holds.

–

Broadcast
\texttt{NOK}(i,j,q_{i}(\alpha_{j}))
, if
q_{ji}\neq q_{i}(\alpha_{j})
holds.

\bullet

Local Computation — Constructing Consistency Graph
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
does the following.

–

Construct a
consistency graph

G_{i}
over
\mathcal{P}
, where the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
is included in

G_{i}
, if
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
is received from the broadcast of
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively, either through the regular-mode or fall-back mode.

\bullet

Phase IV — Constructing
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
:
\mathsf{D}
does the following in its consistency graph

G_{\mathsf{D}}
at time
2\Delta+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.

–

Remove edges incident with
P_{i}
, if
\texttt{NOK}(i,j,q_{ij})
is received from the broadcast of
P_{i}
through regular-mode
and
q_{ij}\neq Q(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
.

–

Set
\mathcal{W}=\{P_{i}:\mathsf{deg}(P_{i})\geq n-t_{s}\}
, where
\mathsf{deg}(P_{i})
denotes the degree of
P_{i}
in
G_{\mathsf{D}}
.

–

Remove
P_{i}
from
\mathcal{W}
, if
P_{i}
is
not
incident with at least
n-t_{s}
parties in
\mathcal{W}
. Repeat this step till no more parties can be
removed from
\mathcal{W}
.

–

Run algorithm
\mathsf{AlgStar}
on
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
, where
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
denotes the subgraph of
G_{\mathsf{D}}
induced by the vertices in
\mathcal{W}
.
If an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
, say
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, is obtained, then broadcast
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.

\bullet

Local Computation — Verifying and Accepting
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
: Each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
does the following at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.

–

If a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is received from
\mathsf{D}
’s broadcast through regular-mode, then
accept
it if following
were true
at time

\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
:

–

There exist no
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
, such that

\texttt{NOK}(j,k,q_{jk})
and
\texttt{NOK}(k,j,q_{kj})
messages
were
received from the broadcast of
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively through regular-mode,
where
q_{jk}\neq q_{kj}
.

–

In the consistency graph
G_{i}
,

\mathsf{deg}(P_{j})\geq n-t_{s}
for all
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
.

–

In the consistency graph
G_{i}
, every
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
has
edges with at least
n-t_{s}
parties from
\mathcal{W}
.

–

(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})

was
an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
in the induced graph
G_{i}[\mathcal{W}]
.

–

For every
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
where the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
is present in
G_{i}
, the
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
messages
were
received from the
broadcast of
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively, through regular-mode.

\bullet

Phase V — Deciding Whether to Go for
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
: At time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, each party
P_{i}
participates in an instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
with input
b_{i}=0
if a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is accepted, else with input
b_{i}=1
and
waits for time
T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

\bullet

Local Computation — Computing VSS-Share Through
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
:
If
0
is the output during
the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
, then each
P_{i}
does the following.

–

If a
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is not yet received, then
wait till it is received from
\mathsf{D}
’s broadcast through fall-back mode.

–

If
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
, then output
q_{i}(0)
.

–

Else, initialize
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
to
\emptyset
. Include
P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}
to
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
if a wps-share
q_{ji}
is computed during

\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
. Wait till
|\mathcal{SS}_{i}|\geq t_{s}+1
. Then interpolate
\{(\alpha_{j},q_{ji})\}_{P_{j}\in\mathcal{SS}_{i}}
to get a
t_{s}
-degree
polynomial, say
q_{i}(x)
, and output
q_{i}(0)
.

\bullet

Phase VI — Broadcasting
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
: If the output during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is
1
,
then
\mathsf{D}
runs
\mathsf{AlgStar}
after every update in its consistency graph
G_{\mathsf{D}}
and broadcasts
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
, if it finds an

(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
.

\bullet

Local Computation — Computing VSS-Share Through
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
: If the output during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is

1
, then each
P_{i}
does the following.

–

Participate in any instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
invoked by
\mathsf{D}
for broadcasting an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}

only
after time

\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
. Wait till some
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
is obtained from
\mathsf{D}
’s broadcast (through any mode), which constitutes an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in
G_{i}
.

–

If
P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, then output
q_{i}(0)
.
Else, include
P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
to
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
(initialized to
\emptyset
) if a wps-share
q_{ji}
is computed in

\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
. Wait till
|\mathcal{SS}_{i}|\geq t_{s}+1
. Then interpolate
\{(\alpha_{j},q_{ji})\}_{P_{j}\in\mathcal{SS}_{i}}
to get a
t_{s}
-degree polynomial
q_{i}(x)
and output
q_{i}(0)
.

Figure 4
:
best-of-both-worlds VSS protocol for a single polynomial.

We next proceed to prove the properties of the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
. We first start by showing that if
\mathsf{D}
is
honest
, then the view of the adversary remains independent of
dealer’s polynomial.

Lemma 4.9

(
t_{s}
-Privacy)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is honest, then irrespective of the network type, the view of the adversary remains independent of
q(\cdot)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
honest
. We consider the worst case scenario when adversary controls up to
t_{s}
parties.
We claim that throughout the protocol, the adversary learns at most
t_{s}
univariate polynomials lying on
Q(x,y)
.
Since
Q(x,y)
is a random
(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree
symmetric-bivariate polynomial, it then follows from Lemma
2.2
,
that the view of the adversary will be independent of
q(\cdot)
. We next proceed to prove the claim.

Corresponding to every
corrupt

P_{i}
, the adversary learns
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
.
Corresponding to every
honest

P_{i}
, the adversary learns
t_{s}
number of
q_{i}(\alpha_{j})
values through pair-wise consistency tests,
as these values are computed as wps-shares, during the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(i)}
.
However, these values are already included in the view of the adversary (through the univariate polynomials under adversary’s control).
Additionally, from the
t_{s}
-privacy
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
, the view of the adversary remains independent of
q_{i}(x)
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(i)}
, if
P_{i}
is
honest
.
Hence no additional information about the polynomials of the honest parties is revealed during the pair-wise consistency checks.
Furthermore, no
honest

P_{i}
ever broadcasts
\texttt{NOK}(i,j,q_{ij})
corresponding to
any
honest

P_{j}
, since the pair-wise consistency check will always pass for every pair of
honest
parties.
∎

We next prove the correctness property in a
synchronous
network.

Lemma 4.10

(
t_{s}
-Correctness)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is honest and network is synchronous, then each honest
P_{i}
outputs
q(\alpha_{i})
within time

T_{\mathsf{VSS}}=\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
honest
and network be
synchronous
with up to
t_{s}
corruptions. During phase I,
all honest parties receive
q_{i}(x)=Q(x,\alpha_{i})
from
\mathsf{D}
within time
\Delta
. Consequently during phase II,
each
honest

P_{i}
invokes the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(i)}
with input

q_{i}(x)
. From the
t_{s}
-correctness
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
in the
synchronous
network,
corresponding to each
honest

P_{j}
, every
honest

P_{i}
computes the wps-share
q_{ji}=q_{j}(\alpha_{i})
, at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}
.
Consequently, during phase III,
every
honest
party broadcasts an
OK
message for every other

honest
party, since
q_{ji}=q_{i}(\alpha_{j})
holds, for every pair of honest parties
P_{i},P_{j}
.
From the
t_{s}
-validity
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network, these
OK
messages are received by
every honest party through regular-mode at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Hence, there will be an edge between every pair of

honest
parties in the consistency graph of every honest party.
Moreover, if
\mathsf{D}
receives an
incorrect

\texttt{NOK}(i,j,q_{ij})
message from the broadcast of any
corrupt

P_{j}
through regular-mode at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
where
q_{ij}\neq Q(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
, then
\mathsf{D}
removes all the edges incident with

P_{i}
in
\mathsf{D}
’s consistency graph
G_{\mathsf{D}}
.

\mathsf{D}
then computes the set
\mathcal{W}
and all
honest
parties will be present in
\mathcal{W}
. Moreover, the honest parties will
form a clique of size at least
n-t_{s}
in the subgraph
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Hence,
\mathsf{D}
will find an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}

(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
in
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
and broadcast
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
during phase IV.
By the
t_{s}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network,
all honest parties will receive
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
through regular-mode at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Moreover, all honest parties will accept
accept

(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
and participate with input
0
in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
By the
t_{s}
-validity
and
t_{s}
-guaranteed liveness
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network,
the output of the
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
instance will be
0
for every honest party at time

\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Now consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. Since
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
,
P_{i}
outputs
s_{i}=q_{i}(0)=Q(0,\alpha_{i})=q(\alpha_{i})
.
∎

We next prove the correctness property in the asynchronous network.

Lemma 4.11

(
t_{a}
-Correctness)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is honest and network is asynchronous, then almost-surely,
each honest
P_{i}
eventually outputs

q(\alpha_{i})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
honest
and network be
asynchronous
with up to
t_{a}
corruptions. We first note that every
honest

P_{i}
eventually broadcasts
\texttt{OK}(i,j)
message, corresponding to every

honest

P_{j}
. This is because both
P_{i}
and
P_{j}
eventually receive
q_{i}(x)=Q(x,\alpha_{i})

and
q_{j}(x)=Q(x,\alpha_{j})
respectively from
\mathsf{D}
. Moreover,
P_{j}

participates with input
q_{j}(\cdot)
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
. And from the
t_{a}
-correctness
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
in the

asynchronous
network,
party
P_{i}
eventually computes the wps-share
q_{ji}=q_{j}(\alpha_{i})
during

\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
. Moreover,
q_{ji}=q_{ij}
holds.
Note that every honest party participates with some input in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
at local time

\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Hence, from the
t_{a}
-almost-surely liveness
and

t_{a}
-consistency
properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the asynchronous network,
almost-surely, all honest parties eventually compute a common output during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Now there are two possible cases:

–

The output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is
0
:
From the
t_{a}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network, this means that at least one
honest
party, say

P_{h}
, participated with input
0
during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
. This implies that

P_{h}

has received
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode and accepted it.
Hence, by the
t_{a}
-weak validity
and
t_{a}
-fallback validity
of

\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network,
all honest parties will eventually receive
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
.
We claim that
every honest

P_{i}
will eventually get
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
.
This will imply that eventually every
honest

P_{i}
outputs
s_{i}=Q(0,\alpha_{i})=q(\alpha_{i})
.
To prove the claim, consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. There are two possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
: In this case,
P_{i}
already has
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
, received from
\mathsf{D}
.

–

P_{i}\notin\mathcal{W}
: In this case, we first note that there will be at least
t_{s}+1
parties, who are eventually included in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
.
This follows from the fact that there are at least

t_{s}+1

honest
parties
P_{j}
in
\mathcal{F}
. And corresponding to every
honest

P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}
, party
P_{i}
will eventually compute the wps-share

q_{ji}
in the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
, which follows from the
t_{a}
-correctness
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
in the
asynchronous
network.
We next claim that corresponding to
every

P_{j}\in\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, the value
q_{ji}
computed by
P_{i}
is the
same
as

Q(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
.

The claim is obviously true for every
honest

P_{j}\in\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, so consider a
corrupt

P_{j}\in\mathcal{SS}_{i}
. We first note that
the input polynomial
q_{j}(x)
of
P_{j}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}

is the
same
as
Q(x,\alpha_{j})
. This is because
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
, since
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathcal{W}
.
And hence
P_{j}
has edges with at least
n-t_{s}
parties in
\mathcal{W}

and hence with at least
n-t_{s}-t_{a}>t_{s}

honest

parties from
\mathcal{W}

in
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
. Let
\mathcal{H}
be the set of
honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
with which
P_{j}
has edges in
G_{\mathsf{D}}[\mathcal{W}]
.
This implies that
every

P_{k}\in\mathcal{H}
has broadcast
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
message after verifying that
q_{jk}=q_{k}(\alpha_{j})
holds,
where
the polynomial
q_{k}(x)
held by
P_{k}
is the same as
Q(x,\alpha_{k})
and where
the WPS-share
q_{jk}
computed by
P_{k}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
is the
same
as
q_{j}(\alpha_{k})
;
the last property follows from the
t_{a}
-strong commitment
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
in the
synchronous
network.
Since
|\mathcal{H}|>t_{s}
, it implies that at least
t_{s}+1

honest
parties
P_{k}
have verified that
q_{j}(\alpha_{k})=Q(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k})

holds. This further implies that
q_{j}(x)=Q(x,\alpha_{j})
, since two different
t_{s}
-degree polynomials can have at most
t_{s}
common values.
Since
P_{i}
has computed the wps-share
q_{ji}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
, from the
t_{a}
-strong commitment
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
in
synchronous
network, it follows that

q_{ji}=q_{j}(\alpha_{i})=Q(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j})=Q(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
. The last equality follows since each
Q(x,y)
is a symmetric bivariate polynomial.

–

The output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is
1
: As mentioned earlier, since
\mathsf{D}
is
honest
, every pair of honest parties eventually
broadcast
OK
messages corresponding to each other, as the pair-wise consistency check between them will be eventually positive.
From the
t_{a}
-weak validity
and
t_{a}
-fallback validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network, these messages are
eventually delivered to every honest party.
Also from the
t_{a}
-weak consistency
and
t_{a}
-fallback consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network,
any
OK
message which is received by
\mathsf{D}
, will be eventually received by every other honest party as well.
As there will be at least
n-t_{a}
honest parties, a clique of size at least
n-t_{a}
will eventually form in the consistency graph of every honest party.
Hence
\mathsf{D}
will eventually find an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
, say
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
, in its consistency graph and broadcast it. From
the
t_{a}
-weak validity
and
t_{a}
-fallback validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network,

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
will be eventually received by every honest party.
Moreover,
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
will be eventually an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in every honest party’s consistency graph.
We now claim that
every honest

P_{i}
will eventually get
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
.
This will imply that eventually every
honest

P_{i}
outputs
s_{i}=Q(0,\alpha_{i})=q(\alpha_{i})
.
To prove the claim, consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. There are two possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case,
P_{i}
already has
Q(x,\alpha_{i})
, received from
\mathsf{D}
.

–

P_{i}\notin\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case, we note that there will be will be at least
t_{s}+1
parties, who are eventually included in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
,
such that
corresponding to
every

P_{j}\in\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, the value
q_{ji}
computed by
P_{i}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
is the same as

Q(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
. The proof for this will be similar as for the case when
P_{i}\notin\mathcal{W}
and the output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is
0
and so we skip the proof.

∎

Before we proceed to prove the
strong commitment
property in the
synchronous
network, we prove a helping lemma.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be corrupt and network be synchronous. If any honest party receives a

(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode and accepts
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
,
then all the following hold:

–

All honest parties in
\mathcal{W}
have received their respective
t_{s}
-degree univariate polynomials from
\mathsf{D}
within time
\Delta
.

–

The univariate polynomials
q_{i}(x)
of all honest parties
P_{i}
in
\mathcal{W}
lie on a unique

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.

–

At time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, every honest party accepts
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
corrupt
and network be
synchronous
with up to
t_{s}
corruptions. As per the lemma condition, let
P_{h}
be an
honest
party, who
receives some
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode and accepts
it at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. From the protocol steps, it then follows that the following must be
true for
P_{h}
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
:

–

There does not exist any

P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
,
such that
\texttt{NOK}(j,k,q_{jk})
and
\texttt{NOK}(k,j,q_{kj})
messages
were
received from the broadcast of

P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively through regular-mode, such that
q_{jk}\neq q_{kj}
.

–

In
P_{h}
’s consistency graph
G_{h}
,
\mathsf{deg}(P_{j})\geq n-t_{s}
for all
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
and
P_{j}
has edges with at least

n-t_{s}
parties from
\mathcal{W}
.

–

(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})

was
an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
in the induced subgraph
G_{h}[\mathcal{W}]
, such that
for every
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
where the edge
(P_{j},P_{k})
is present in
G_{h}
, the
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
messages
were
received from the broadcast of

P_{j}
and
P_{k}
respectively through regular-mode.

We prove the first part of the lemma through a contradiction. So let
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
be an
honest
party, who receives its

t_{s}
-degree univariate polynomial from
\mathsf{D}
, say
q_{j}(x)
, at time
\Delta+\delta
, where

\delta>0
. Moreover, let
P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
be an
honest
party such that
P_{j}
has an edge with
P_{k}

(note that
P_{j}
has edges with at least
n-2t_{s}>t_{s}+t_{a}

honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
).
As stated above, at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, party
P_{h}
has received the message
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
from the broadcast of
P_{k}
through regular-mode.
From the protocol steps,
P_{j}
waits till its local time becomes a multiple of
\Delta
, before it participates with input
q_{j}(\cdot)
in the instance

\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
. Hence,
P_{j}
must have invoked
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
at time
c\cdot\Delta
, where
c\geq 2
.
Since the network is
synchronous
, from the
t_{s}
-correctness
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
in the
synchronous
network, party
P_{k}
will compute its wps-share
q_{jk}

during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
at time
c\cdot\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}
. Hence the result of the pair-wise consistency test with
P_{j}
will be available to
P_{k}
at time

c\cdot\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}
. As a result,
P_{k}
starts broadcasting
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
message only at time
c\cdot\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}
.
Since
P_{k}
is
honest
, from the
t_{s}
-validity
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network,
it will take
exactly

T_{\mathsf{BC}}
time for the message
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
to be received through regular-mode, once it is broadcast.
This implies that
P_{h}
will receive the message
\texttt{OK}(k,j)

at time
c\cdot\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, where
c\geq 2
.
However, this is a contradiction, since the
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
message has been received by
P_{h}
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.

To prove the second part of the lemma, we will show that
the univariate polynomials
q_{i}(x)
of all the
honest
parties
P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
are pair-wise consistent.
Since there will be at least
n-2t_{s}>t_{s}

honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
,
from Lemma
2.2
this will imply that all these polynomials
lie on a unique
(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
So consider an arbitrary pair of
honest
parties
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
. From the first part of the claim, both
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
must have received their respective univariate polynomials

q_{j}(x)
and
q_{k}(x)
by time
\Delta
.
Moreover, from the
t_{s}
-correctness
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
in the
synchronous
network,
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
will compute the wps-shares

q_{kj}=q_{k}(\alpha_{j})
and
q_{jk}=q_{j}(\alpha_{k})
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(k)}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}

respectively. Since
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
are honest,
if
q_{kj}\neq q_{jk}
, they would broadcast
\texttt{NOK}(j,k,q_{jk})
and
\texttt{NOK}(k,j,q_{kj})
messages respectively at
time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}
. From the
t_{s}
-validity
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network,
P_{h}
will receive these messages through regular-mode at
time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Consequently,
P_{h}

will not
accept
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, which is a contradiction.

To prove the third part of the lemma, we note that since
P_{h}
receives

(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, it implies
that
\mathsf{D}
must have started broadcasting
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
latest at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. This is because
it takes
T_{\mathsf{BC}}
time for the regular-mode of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
to generate an output. From the

t_{s}
-consistency
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network, it follows that every honest party will also receive
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})

from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Similarly, since at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
, party
P_{h}
has received the
\texttt{OK}(j,k)
and
\texttt{OK}(k,j)
messages
through regular-mode
from the broadcast of
every
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{W}
where
(P_{j},P_{k})
is an edge in
P_{h}
’s consistency graph, it follows that these messages started getting broadcast latest
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}
.
From the
t_{s}
-validity

and
t_{s}
-consistency
properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network, it follows that every honest party receives these broadcast messages through
regular-mode at time

\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Hence
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
will constitute an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
in the induced subgraph
G_{i}[\mathcal{W}]
of every honest party
P_{i}
’s
consistency-graph at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+T_{\mathsf{BC}}

and consequently, every honest party accepts
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.
∎

We next prove the strong commitment property in the synchronous network.

Lemma 4.13

(
t_{s}
-Strong Commitment)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is corrupt and network is synchronous, then either no honest party computes any output or there exist a

t_{s}
-degree polynomial, say
q^{\star}(\cdot)
, such that each honest
P_{i}
eventually outputs
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
, where the following hold.

–

If any honest
P_{i}
computes its output at time
T_{\mathsf{VSS}}=\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
,
then every honest party obtains its output at time
T_{\mathsf{VSS}}
.

–

If any honest
P_{i}
computes its output at time
T
where
T>T_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, then every honest party computes its output by time

T+2\Delta
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
corrupt
and network be
synchronous
with up to
t_{s}
corruptions.
If no honest party computes any output, then the lemma holds trivially. So consider the case when some
honest
party
computes an output.
Now, there are two possible cases.

–

At least one honest party, say
P_{h}
, has received some

(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode and accepted
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
:
In this case, from Lemma
4.12
, the polynomials
q_{i}(x)
of all
honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
are guaranteed to lie on a unique

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say

Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
As per the protocol steps,
P_{h}
has also verified that
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathcal{W}
, by checking that
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
constitutes an
(n,t_{s}){-}\mbox{star}
in
the induced subgraph
G_{h}[\mathcal{W}]
.
Hence the polynomials
q_{i}(x)

of all
honest
parties in
\mathcal{F}
also lie on
Q^{\star}(x,y)
. Moreover, from Lemma
4.12
, all honest parties
accept
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Hence, every honest party participates in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
with input
0
. Consequently, by the
t_{s}
-validity
and
t_{s}
-guaranteed liveness
properties of

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
synchronous
network,
all honest parties compute the output
0
during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
at time
T_{\mathsf{VSS}}=\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Let
q^{\star}(\cdot)=Q^{\star}(0,y)
and consider an arbitrary
honest

party
P_{i}
. We wish to show that
P_{i}
has
q_{i}(x)=Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
at time
T_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, which will imply that
P_{i}
outputs

s_{i}=q_{i}(0)
at time
T_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, which will be the same as
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.
For this, we consider the following two possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
: In this case,
P_{i}
has already received
q_{i}(x)
from
\mathsf{D}
within time
\Delta
.
This follows from Lemma
4.12
.

–

P_{i}\notin\mathcal{W}
: In this case, we claim that at time
T_{\mathsf{VSS}}
,
there will be will be at least
t_{s}+1
parties from
\mathcal{F}
, who are included in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, such that
corresponding to
every

P_{j}\in\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, party
P_{i}
will have the value
q_{ji}
, which will be the same as
Q^{\star}(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
.
Namely, there are at least
t_{s}+1

honest
parties in
\mathcal{F}
, who will be included in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}

and the claim will be trivially true for those parties, due to the
t_{s}
-correctness
property of
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
in the synchronous network.
On the other hand, if any
corrupt

P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}
is included in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, then
the input polynomial of
P_{j}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
will be pair-wise consistent with the polynomials of at least

t_{s}+1

honest
parties in
\mathcal{W}
and hence will be the same as
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{j})
. Moreover,
from the
t_{s}
-weak commitment
of

\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}
in the
synchronous
network, the WPS-share
q_{ji}
computed by
P_{i}
during
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}

will be the same as
Q^{\star}(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j})
, which will be the same as
Q^{\star}(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
, since

Q^{\star}(x,y)
is a symmetric bivariate polynomial.
Hence,
P_{i}
will interpolate
q_{i}(x)
.

–

No honest party has received any

(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode and accepted
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
:
This implies that all honest parties participate in the instance of

\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
with input
1
. Hence, by the
t_{s}
-validity
and
t_{s}
-guaranteed liveness
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in
the
synchronous
network,
all honest parties obtain the output
1
during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
at time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}+T_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Let
P_{h}
be the
first honest
party, who computes an output.
This means that
P_{h}
has received a pair
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
, such that
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
constitutes an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}

in
P_{h}
’s consistency graph. Let
T
be the time when
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
constitutes an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in
P_{h}
’s consistency graph.
This implies that at time
T
, party
P_{h}
has
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
from
\mathsf{D}
’s broadcast and also all the
\texttt{OK}(\star,\star)
messages,
from the broadcast of respective parties in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
.
From the protocol steps,
T>T_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, since the honest parties participate in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
through which

\mathsf{D}
has broadcast
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})

only
after time
T_{\mathsf{VSS}}
.
By the
t_{s}
-consistency
and
t_{s}
-fallback consistency
properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
synchronous
network,

all
honest parties will receive
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})

from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
by time
T+2\Delta
. Moreover,

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
will constitute an
(n,t_{a}){-}\mbox{star}
in every honest party’s consistency graph by time
T+2\Delta
.
This is because all the
OK
messages which are received by
P_{h}
from the broadcast of various parties in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}

are guaranteed to be received by every honest party by time
T+2\Delta
.
Since
|\mathcal{E}^{\prime}|\geq n-2t_{a}>2t_{s}+(t_{s}-t_{a})>2t_{s}
, it follows that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
has at least
t_{s}+1

honest
parties.
Moreover,
the univariate polynomials
(q_{j}(x),q_{k}(x))
of every pair of
honest
parties
P_{j},P_{k}\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
will be pair-wise consistent
and hence lie on a unique

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
Similarly, the univariate polynomial
q_{i}(x)
of every
honest
party
P_{i}
in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is pair-wise consistent with the univariate polynomials

q_{j}(x)
of
all
the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}

and hence lie on
Q^{\star}(x,y)
as well.
Let
q^{\star}(\cdot)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}Q^{\star}(0,y)
. We show that
every honest

P_{i}
outputs
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
, by time
T+2\Delta
.
For this it is enough to show that each honest
P_{i}
has
q_{i}(x)=Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
by time
T+2\Delta
,
as
P_{i}
outputs
q_{i}(0)
, which will be the same as
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.
Consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. There are two possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case,
P_{i}
has already received
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
from
\mathsf{D}
, well before time
T+2\Delta
.

–

P_{i}\notin\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
: In this case, we claim that by time
T+2\Delta
,
there will be will be at least
t_{s}+1
parties from
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, who are included in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, such that
corresponding to
every

P_{j}\in\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, party
P_{i}
will have the value
q_{ji}
, which will be the same as
Q^{\star}(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
.
The proof for this is very similar to the previous case when
P_{i}\notin\mathcal{W}
and the output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is
0
.
Namely every
honest

P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
will be included in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
. This is because
P_{j}
starts broadcasting
OK
messages for other parties in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}

only after invoking instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
with input
q_{j}(x)
.
Hence, by time
T+2\Delta
, the WPS-share
q_{ji}
from the instance
\Pi_{\mathsf{WPS}}^{(j)}
will be available with
P_{i}
.
On the other hand, if a
corrupt

P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is included in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
, then also the claim holds (the proof for this is similar to the proof of
the
t_{a}
-correctness
property in the
asynchronous
network in Lemma
4.11
).

∎

We finally prove the strong commitment property in an asynchronous network.

Lemma 4.14

(
t_{a}
-Strong Commitment)
.

In protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
, if
\mathsf{D}
is corrupt and network is asynchronous, then either no honest party computes any output or there exist some

t_{s}
-degree polynomial, say
q^{\star}(\cdot)
, such that almost-surely, every honest
P_{i}
eventually outputs
q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{D}
be
corrupt
and the network be
asynchronous
with up to
t_{a}
corruptions. If no honest party computes any output, then the lemma holds trivially. So, consider the case when some honest party
computes an output. We note that every
honest
party participates with some input in the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
at local time

\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
. Hence, from the
t_{a}
-almost-surely liveness
and
t_{a}
-consistency
properties of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}

in the asynchronous network,
almost-surely, all honest parties eventually compute a common output during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
Now there are two possible cases:

–

The output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is 0
:
From the
t_{a}
-validity
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
in the
asynchronous
network, it follows that at least one honest party, say
P_{h}
,
participated with input
0
during the instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
.
This means that
P_{h}
has received some

(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
through regular-mode
and accepted it at local time
\Delta+T_{\mathsf{WPS}}+2T_{\mathsf{BC}}
.
Hence, by the
t_{a}
-weak consistency
and
t_{a}
-fallback consistency
of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BC}}
in the
asynchronous
network,
all honest parties will eventually receive
(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
from the broadcast of
\mathsf{D}
.
There will be at least
n-2t_{s}-t_{a}>t_{s}

honest
parties
P_{i}
in
\mathcal{E}
, whose univariate polynomials
q_{i}(x)
are pair-wise consistent and hence lie on
a unique

(t_{s},t_{s})
-degree symmetric bivariate polynomial, say
Q^{\star}(x,y)
.
Similarly, the univariate polynomial
q_{i}(x)
of every
honest

P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
will be pair-wise consistent with the univariate polynomials
q_{j}(x)
of
all
the
honest
parties
P_{j}
in
\mathcal{E}
and hence will lie on

Q^{\star}(x,y)
as well. Let
q^{\star}(\cdot)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}Q^{\star}(0,y)
.
We claim that
every honest

P_{i}
will eventually have
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
. This will imply that eventually every
honest

P_{i}
outputs

s_{i}=Q^{\star}(0,\alpha_{i})=q^{\star}(\alpha_{i})
.
To prove the claim, consider an arbitrary
honest
party
P_{i}
. There are three possible cases.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
and
P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
: In this case,
P_{i}
has received the polynomials
q_{i}(x)
from
\mathsf{D}
and since

P_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
, the condition
q_{i}(x)=Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
holds.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{W}
and
P_{i}\not\in\mathcal{F}
:
In this case,
P_{i}
has received the polynomial
q_{i}(x)
from
\mathsf{D}
.
Since
|\mathcal{W}|\geq n-t_{s}
and
|\mathcal{F}|\geq n-t_{s}
,
|\mathcal{W}\cap\mathcal{F}|\geq n-2t_{s}>t_{s}+t_{a}
.
From the protocol steps, the polynomial
q_{i}(x)
is pair-wise consistent with the polynomials
q_{j}(x)
at least
n-t_{s}
parties
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}
,
since
P_{i}
has edges with at least
n-t_{s}
parties
P_{j}
within
\mathcal{W}
. Now among these
n-t_{s}
parties, at least
n-2t_{s}
parties will be from
\mathcal{F}
, of which
at least
n-2t_{s}-t_{a}>t_{s}
parties will be
honest
. Hence,
q_{i}(x)
is pair-wise consistent with the
q_{j}(x)
polynomials of at least
t_{s}+1

honest
parties
P_{j}\in\mathcal{F}
. Now since the
q_{j}(x)
polynomials of all the
honest
parties in
\mathcal{F}
lie on
Q^{\star}(x,y)
, it implies that

q_{i}(x)=Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
holds.

–

P_{i}\notin\mathcal{W}
: In this case, similar to the proof of Lemma
4.11
, one can show that

P_{i}
eventually includes at least
t_{s}+1
parties from
\mathcal{F}
in
\mathcal{SS}_{i}
.
And the value computed by
P_{i}
corresponding to

any

P_{j}\in\mathcal{SS}_{i}
will be the same as
Q^{\star}(\alpha_{j},\alpha_{i})
.
Hence,
P_{i}
will eventually interpolate
Q^{\star}(x,\alpha_{i})
.

–

The output of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BA}}
is 1
: Let
P_{h}
be the
first honest
party, who computes an output in
\Pi_{\mathsf{VSS}}
.
This means that
P_{h}
